### §5.1 `P-PUB-1` — the resolution order is the declared order

**⟶ THE REGISTER IS EXTENDED HERE BY TWO ROWS, DECLARED AS ROWS OF THIS UNIT'S OWN REGISTER AND NOT AS A SECOND ONE, 2026-10-12 BY THE `U-STOREROOM-CONSUMER-FIX` SPEC PASS (`role_spec_writer`), ON `A91`.** `RCA-8(d)` ANNOTATE-BESIDE: **`§5.0`'s census paragraphs, `§5.0`'s own dated count clause and every one of `§5.1`–`§5.8` STANDS BYTE-FOR-BYTE and is read THROUGH this block; THIS block adds TWO ROWS, TWO STRATEGY IDS and ONE CAP AMENDMENT, mints no `F-` row and no `§6`/`FS-n` form, renumbers no pre-pass row and EMPTIES NO ROW'S FAILURE LIST.** **THIS PASS RAN NOTHING: no `cargo`, no `npm`, no `node`.**

**AND THE CAP'S OWN AMENDMENT, STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE A CAP IS NOT SILENTLY EXCEEDED: THIS UNIT'S REGISTER FAMILY MOVES `8 → 10` ROWS (`8 + 2 = 10` ✓), AND ITS CAP MOVES WITH IT, FROM `≤ 8` TO `≤ 10`, HOLDING AT `10 ≤ 10` ✓.** **THE AMENDMENT'S REASON, WITH THE ALTERNATIVE PRICED: `§0.1` ITEM `5` AND THE INDEX REGION'S SCOPE BOUNDARY EACH FORBID *"a second typed register in this directory or in any other"*, so the two rows this pass owes CANNOT be carried as a second surface; and OMITTING THEM IS NOT AVAILABLE EITHER, because a register with no row needs a recorded zero-row rationale and this unit has none — the stamp's equality and the guard's firability are exactly the properties no existing row can carry.** **THE OPERATIVE RANGE IS `P-PUB-1`…`P-PUB-10`, every row is FAILABLE, no row's failure list is empty, every row cites at least one strategy id, and every strategy id is cited by at least one row.** **AND THE ALTERNATIVE — leaving the cap at `8` and writing the two properties as unregistered prose — IS PRICED: the two properties would then be carried by NO row, `un-run` would have no row to fail as `un-run`, and the two ids `S-STAMP`/`S-GUARD` would have no citing row; available only with a fresh ruling recorded as an ACTIVE `docs/decisions.md` row, followed by a bounded amendment of `§0.1` item `5`, of the index region's scope boundary and of this block together.** `[A2 docs/decisions.md · the ACTIVE `A91` row of 2026-10-12; A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish/release.md §2.4` and `§1.4a`; A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §0.1` item `5` | numeric or census claim]`

**⟶ AND THE REGISTER'S CULL, WHICH IS THIS GATE'S OWN ACT AND NOT A CAP-FITTING DROP, IS DISPOSED HERE, DATED `2026-10-13` BY THE GATE-2 EIGHT-FINDING CLOSE-OUT PASS (`role_spec_writer`).** `RCA-8(d)` ANNOTATE-BESIDE: **the block above stands BYTE-FOR-BYTE and is read THROUGH this clause; THIS clause adds no row, drops no row, edits no row's property, strategies or failure list, and re-decides no ruling — it DISPOSES OF A CANDIDATE SET the register never carried.** **THE CANDIDATE SET, AS THE SUPERVISOR'S GATE-1 FILING RETURNS IT: `16` candidate property rows, `IM-1`…`IM-7` · `SM-1`…`SM-4` · `TP-1`…`TP-5`, against a cap of `10`.** **AND ONE INSTRUMENT'S LIMIT IS STATED WITH THE CULL, BECAUSE A CULL READ OFF THE WRONG INSTRUMENT IS THE CLASS THIS PROJECT HAS PAID FOR SIX TIMES: THE CANDIDATE SET IS REACHABLE AT ITS IDS AND AT THREE MARKS ONLY — the ids and the marks are quoted from the supervisor's own filing, and the candidates' ROW TEXT IS CARRIED BY NO FILE THIS PASS CAN OPEN, the process-rule spec that would enumerate them not being filed under this repository.** **SO THIS CULL IS A DISPOSITION RECORD AND NOT A TEXT DELETION, AND IT CLAIMS NO FIGURE IT DID NOT READ.** **THE FIVE DISPOSITIONS, WITH THE ARITHMETIC PRINTED SO NO TERM IS INVISIBLE: `16` candidates = `1` OUTSIDE-THE-REGISTER-SUBJECT DISPOSAL + `2` IDENTIFIED-BUT-UNRECOVERED CANDIDATES FOLDED TO THEIR ROW'S SUBJECT + `2` RECORDED ZERO-ROW RATIONALES + `5` FOLDED INTO OPERATIVE ROWS = `10` OPERATIVE ROWS, WHICH IS THE CAP — `1 + 2 + 2 + 5 = 10` ✓, `10 ≤ 10` ✓.**

#### `§5.0a` `P-PUB-9` — every released native artefact's `version()` answers the main package manifest's `version`, character for character

- **The property:** **every released native artefact's `version()` answers the main package manifest's `version` CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER, AND THE EQUALITY IS ESTABLISHED AT BUILD TIME BY A STAMP TAKEN FROM THAT SAME MANIFEST RATHER THAN BY ANY RUN-TIME NEGOTIATION.** **The value's source, its carrier and its fallback are declared at `release.md §2.4` and `release.md §6.10`, and the consumed value's semantics row is `PAR-PUB-35`.**
- **Strategies:** `S-STAMP` · `S-VERSION`.
- **Its failure list, and `un-run` is a FAILURE:** **a released artefact whose `version()` answers the crate's `Cargo.toml` version** — the absent-manifest fallback reaching a release; **a `version()` answering an empty string**; **a `version()` answering a value that is not a `string`**; **a `version` that is not callable at all** (so the read answers `undefined`, which is a non-string and therefore the arm); **an artefact whose stamped version differs from its own manifest's by one character**; **a substitute artefact loaded in place of the one built from these sources**; **a carrier that stamps a value while declaring NO re-run trigger on the manifest it reads** — the warm-`target/` state in which the artefact ships the PREVIOUS version; **a carrier that falls back from an EMPTY string**; **a carrier that validates the stamped value as a semver at a third site**; **a carrier that reads an ambient environment variable at all** (the refused workflow-env form); and **`un-run` on any of the above**.
- **Firability, and the one cell that is not locally firable, stated so the row is not read as wider than it is:** **the two `cargo:` re-run directives, the directive's exact name, the absence of an ambient read and the artefact's own self-report are ALL firable at the local build, so this row's drive does not need a release; while the absent-manifest-in-a-RELEASE case is firable by NO cell of this unit's checkout and is observed by the guard step `P-PUB-10` ALONE, at the leg that built the artefact.**
`[A2 docs/decisions.md · the ACTIVE `A91` row of 2026-10-12; A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish/release.md §2.4` and `§6.10`; A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish/parameter-semantics.md §5.1`; read with `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §1.5` | state or fail-state declaration]`

#### `§5.0b` `P-PUB-10` — the pre-publish version guard is declared, per leg, before the version is spent

- **The property:** **the RELEASE declares a step that, FOR EACH OF THE FIVE PLATFORM LEGS, LOADS THAT LEG'S OWN ASSEMBLED ARTEFACT AND COMPARES ITS `version()` AGAINST THAT LEG'S OWN PLATFORM MANIFEST'S `version`, AND THE STEP STANDS BEFORE THE FIRST PUBLISH SITE SO A MISMATCH FAILS THE RUN BEFORE THE VERSION IS SPENT.** **The step, its position, its subject and its ordering are declared at `release.md §1.4a`.**
- **Strategies:** `S-GUARD` · `S-VERSION`.
- **Its failure list, and `un-run` is a FAILURE:** **the guard step absent from the workflow file**; **the step declared but placed AFTER a publish site**; **a step that reads the MAIN package's manifest alone rather than the leg's own platform manifest**; **a step whose subject is the checkout's own `build/Release` artefact** — a path a consumer's install cannot reach, so the guard would assert nothing about the released bytes; **a step that compares a COMPUTED string rather than the artefact's own self-report**; **a step whose failure does not fail the run** (a warning, a `continue-on-error`, or a swallowed non-zero exit); **a guard set that covers `k` of the five legs**; and **`un-run` on any of the above.**
- **And the two drives this row does and does not admit, stated because the row's own firability is a fact of the red set's reach:** **the step's EXISTENCE, its POSITION and its SUBJECT are firable by a cell that reads the workflow file; the COMPARISON itself is NOT firable by any cell of `bindings/js/test/publish.test.mjs`, because a cell cannot load a foreign-target `.node` — its drive is the RUN, at the leg that built the artefact, and that is why this row is declared rather than left to a drive.**
`[A2 docs/decisions.md · the ACTIVE `A91` row of 2026-10-12; A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish/release.md §1.4a`; read with `§3.1`'s seventh-step clause, `§5.0a` and `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/acceptance.md §3.1`'s row-`4` clause | requirement]`

**Property.** **Rung one `require`s the scoped name the closed key set declares for the runtime's pair; failing it, rung two `require`s the module-relative `build/Release/storeroom_js_binding.node`; failing it, the load path answers the declared absent-addon form. NO rung is skipped, reordered or short-circuited, and rung two is never reported as rung one.**
**Strategy ids.** **`S-RESOLVE`** (one drive per rung, **the rung observed from outside** the entry) · **`S-INPLACE`** (the existing JS leg's own resolution still resolves at rung two, in place).
**FAILURE LIST.** a rung-one success reported as rung two; a rung-two load that never attempts rung one; **a rung-three answer reached while a rung remains untried**; a candidate list resolved out of order; a covered key whose rung one is skipped; a rung-two path derived from anything but the entry module's own location; a rung-two attempt made AFTER rung one succeeded.
**The clauses it cites.** `§1.1` · `§1.4` · `§1.5` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/resolution.md §2` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §0.3` (`PUB-DD-20`).

### §5.2 `P-PUB-2` — the key set is closed at the five declared names

**Property.** **`A84`'s `C-1`: the entry never `require`s a computed name outside the five, so `W-3` cannot exist — the name is SELECTED from the closed table by the runtime's pair and never BUILT by concatenation at any depth.**
**Strategy ids.** **`S-KEYSET`** (the table's totality over the five names, and its closedness against every name the entry can produce).
**FAILURE LIST.** **any computed name outside the five**; **a name assembled from tokens at any depth of the entry**; a table that falls back to a guessed name on an uncovered pair; **its converse: a declared key the table cannot answer**; a fifth pin expressed as a range rather than an exact version.
**The clauses it cites.** `§1.3` · `§1.2` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/resolution.md §3` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §0.3` (`PUB-DD-10`, `PUB-DD-15`).
`[A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish-proposal.md §9 · `A84`(c) — the closed key set | requirement]`

### §5.3 `P-PUB-3` — the platform key is total and deterministic

**Property.** **`process.platform` × `process.arch` selects at most one of the five declared keys, and every other combination — `linux ia32`, `linux ppc64le`, `linux s390x`, `freebsd`, `win32 ia32`, `sunos`, `android`, and every other pair Node documents — is answered by a declared rung, never by a guess and never by a sixth key; and the musl case's answer is declared.**
**Strategy ids.** **`S-PLATFORM`** (one drive per declared pair, plus one per uncovered combination) · **`S-MUSL`** (the musl answer declared and driven).
**FAILURE LIST.** a speculative sixth key; **a musl runtime that loads a `-gnu` artefact where its `dlopen` should have failed and the answer therefore differed from the declared one**; an uncovered pair whose answer is unstated; **a `libc` detection mechanism, value set or measured domain declared by any clause**; a pair answered by two rungs; a table entry that depends on the process's working directory or an environment variable.
**The clauses it cites.** `§1.2` · `§1.4` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/resolution.md §4` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §0.3` (`PUB-DD-8`).
`[A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish-proposal.md §11 · `A85`(7) and `§10.3` · `Q-7` — the forced answer for musl and the uncovered pairs | requirement]`

### §5.4 `P-PUB-4` — the version read holds

**Property.** **The addon's own version is read at load, is compared with the package's declared version for exact string equality, and a mismatch is answered by the single declared failure arm — never by a fallback — while the read is a read of THIS ARTEFACT'S OWN SELF-REPORT and NOT an ABI probe of the runtime.**
**Strategy ids.** **`S-VERSION`** (a substituted artefact refused by the declared arm, one drive per direction: a differing string and a match) · **`S-INPLACE`** (the rung-two load performs no comparison, so the in-place loop still resolves).
**FAILURE LIST.** a load that performs no version read; **a version read that answers a second failure SHAPE, or mints a status, or falls back**; **a mismatch accepted**; a comparison by range rather than by exact equality; a non-string version coerced into a match; a version read attempted at rung two; a version read on a path where no platform package was resolved; **a mismatch arm whose `message` the ENTRY authored rather than the artefact's own generator** (`§1.4` item 6, arm (a); `PUB-DD-25`).
**The clauses it cites.** `§1.5` · `§1.4` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/resolution.md §5` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §0.3` (`PUB-DD-17`, `PUB-DD-18`, `PUB-DD-19`, `PUB-DD-25`).
`[A1 docs/specs/storeroom-publish-proposal.md §9 · `A84`(b) — the version read and its arm | requirement]`

### §5.5 `P-PUB-5` — the taxonomy is total and the arm is one

**Property.** **Absent · present-unloadable · present-wrong-version · present-right-version, at covered, uncovered and musl keys, each landing on a declared answer; and the single declared failure arm's form and subject are fixed once.**
**Strategy ids.** **`S-TAXONOMY`** (one drive per cell of the `4 × 3` table) · **`S-ONEARM`** (the arm's form, its status and its subject).
**FAILURE LIST.** a state with no answer; **two states with different answers under one name**; a rung one `catch` that classifies the throw and thereby absorbs a broken prebuild into a second failure shape; a second failure shape; a minted status; a subject that is `cfg.filePath` rather than the addon's own path; **a two-path subject joined by any separator but `', '`, or reported in any order but rung one then rung two, or rendered without each path's own apostrophes inside the generator's one slot** (`§1.5` item 6; `PUB-DD-26`); **a `message` that is not the store's generator's string in the arm where the generator WAS reachable, or not the generator's own clause reproduced byte-for-byte in the arm where it was not** (`§1.4` item 6; `PUB-DD-25`); a fallback to a JavaScript store, a partial instance or a no-op verb; an uncovered `present-wrong-version` cell driven as though it were reachable; a fourth outcome.
**The clauses it cites.** `§1.4` · `§1.1` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/resolution.md §5` · `docs/specs/storeroom-publish/contract.md §0.3` (`PUB-DD-17`, `PUB-DD-25`, `PUB-DD-26`).
